=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There exists an undirected tree with n nodes numbered 0 to n - 1. You are given a 2D integer array edges of length n - 1, where edges[i] = [u_i, v_i, w_i] indicates that there is an edge between nodes u_i and v_i with weight w_i in the tree.
Your task is to remove zero or more edges such that:

Each node has an edge with at most k other nodes, where k is given.
The sum of the weights of the remaining edges is maximized.

Return the maximum possible sum of weights for the remaining edges after making the necessary removals.
 
Example 1:

Input: edges = [[0,1,4],[0,2,2],[2,3,12],[2,4,6]], k = 2
Output: 22
Explanation:


Node 2 has edges with 3 other nodes. We remove the edge [0, 2, 2], ensuring that no node has edges with more than k = 2 nodes.
The sum of weights is 22, and we can't achieve a greater sum. Thus, the answer is 22.


Example 2:

Input: edges = [[0,1,5],[1,2,10],[0,3,15],[3,4,20],[3,5,5],[0,6,10]], k = 3
Output: 65
Explanation:

Since no node has edges connecting it to more than k = 3 nodes, we don't remove any edges.
The sum of weights is 65. Thus, the answer is 65.


 
Constraints:

2 <= n <= 10^5
1 <= k <= n - 1
edges.length == n - 1
edges[i].length == 3
0 <= edges[i][0] <= n - 1
0 <= edges[i][1] <= n - 1
1 <= edges[i][2] <= 10^6
The input is generated such that edges form a valid tree.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maximizeSumOfWeights(self, edges: List[List[int]], k: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

            s1 += gains[i]
        else:
            break

return best0[0]

Now, code:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximizeSumOfWeights(self, edges: List[List[int]], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(edges) + 1
        adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for u, v, w in edges:
            adj[u].append((v, w))
            adj[v].append((u, w))
        
        parent = [-1] * n
        order = []
        stack = [0]
        parent[0] = -1
        while stack:
            v = stack.pop()
            order.append(v)
            for to, _ in adj[v]:
                if to == parent[v]:
                    continue
                parent[to] = v
                stack.append(to)
        
        dp0 = [0] * n
        dp1 = [0] * n
        
        for v in reversed(order):
            base = 0
            gains = []
            for to, w in adj[v]:
                if to == parent[v]:
                    continue
                base += dp0[to]
                gains.append(w + dp1[to] - dp0[to])
            gains.sort(reverse=True)
            best0 = base
            best1 = base
            # capacity k and k-1
            # add positive gains in order until capacity exhausted
            for i, g in enumerate(gains):
                if g <= 0 or i >= k:
                    break
                best0 += g
                if i < k - 1:
                    best1 += g
            dp0[v] = best0
            dp1[v] = best1
        
        return dp0[0]
```
Need check `parent[0] = -1`; in loop for root, for to in adj[0], to != -1, set parent[to]=0. Good. But what if a node has neighbor already assigned as parent? In tree no. But consider root's child, when processing root, set parent[child]=0. Later, if root has another neighbor that is also connected to child? That would be cycle, invalid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a more subtle mathematical issue: The DP state F[v][s] = max sum with at most s child edges selected. But for a non-root node, if parent edge selected, the child's degree capacity is k-1. Now, let's maybe derive recurrence in terms of `dp0` and `dp1` more explicitly:
For node v, let `dp0[v]` = best sum in subtree when v has no selected parent edge (so v can have up to k selected edges to children). Let `dp1[v]` = best sum in subtree when v has selected parent edge (so v can have up to k-1 selected edges to children).
For each child u:
- If edge (v,u) is not selected, u has no selected parent edge, so contribution `dp0[u]`.
- If edge is selected, contribution `w + dp1[u]`.
If we start from all not selected, total `sum dp0[u]`. Selecting edge gives delta `w + dp1[u] - dp0[u]`. We can select at most `k` (for dp0) or `k-1` (for dp1) children, and only positive deltas. This is exactly code.

Need maybe if `dp1[u]` for k-1 capacity but if k-1 negative? k>=1, so k-1>=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue

=== ANSWER ===
as a code block only.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximizeSumOfWeights(self, edges: List[List[int]], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(edges) + 1
        adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for u, v, w in edges:
            adj[u].append((v, w))
            adj[v].append((u, w))

        parent = [-1] * n
        order = []
        stack = [0]

        while stack:
            v = stack.pop()
            order.append(v)
            for to, _ in adj[v]:
                if to == parent[v]:
                    continue
                parent[to] = v
                stack.append(to)

        # dp0[v]: best sum in v's subtree if v has no selected parent edge
        #         (v may select up to k child edges)
        # dp1[v]: best sum in v's subtree if v has a selected parent edge
        #         (v may select up to k-1 child edges)
        dp0 = [0] * n
        dp1 = [0] * n

        for v in reversed(order):
            base = 0
            gains = []

            for to, w in adj[v]:
                if to == parent[v]:
                    continue

                base += dp0[to]
                gains.append(w + dp1[to] - dp0[to])

            gains.sort(reverse=True)

            best0 = base
            best1 = base

            for i, g in enumerate(gains):
                if g <= 0 or i >= k:
                    break
                best0 += g
                if i < k - 1:
                    best1 += g

            dp0[v] = best0
            dp1[v] = best1

        return dp0[0]
```
